\widehat{\mathbf{S}}
.
∎

As shown in
[
57
]
, the classifying space functor
\textnormal{B}\colon\mathbf{G}\longrightarrow\mathbf{S}
induces weak homotopy equivalences of topological cyclic operads

\bigl|\textnormal{B}(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\bigr|\ \simeq\ \bigl|\textnormal{B}(\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\bigr|\ \simeq\ \mathsf{S}_{0}.

After geometric realisation, the groupoid models
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
and
\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
thus determine equivalent topological cyclic operads, and the resulting operad
\mathsf{S}_{0}
provides a cyclic topological model for the framed little disks operad.

Theorem 5.11
.

There exists an isomorphism of profinite monoids

\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}\cong\pi_{0}\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\big(\widehat{\textnormal{N}\mathsf{S}}_{0}\big).

Proof.

By Corollaries
5.6
and
5.7
, there are isomorphisms of profinite monoids

\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}\ \cong\ \operatorname{HoEnd}\bigl(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\bigr)\ \cong\ \pi_{0}\,\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Map}\bigl(\textnormal{N}\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}},\textnormal{N}\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\bigr).

The classifying space functor
\textnormal{B}\colon\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{S}
is homotopically fully faithful, yielding a weak equivalence

\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\bigl(\textnormal{N}\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\bigl(\textnormal{B}\big(\textnormal{N}\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\big)\bigr).

Using Lemma
5.10
, we therefore obtain

\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}\ \cong\ \pi_{0}\,\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\bigl(\widehat{\textnormal{B}\big(\textnormal{N}\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}}\big)\bigr)\cong\pi_{0}\,\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\bigl(\widehat{\textnormal{B}\textnormal{N}\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}}\bigr).

By
[
57
]
, after geometric realization there are weak homotopy equivalences of topological cyclic operads

|\textnormal{B}(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})|\ \simeq\ |\textnormal{B}(\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})|\ \simeq\ \mathsf{S}_{0},

and the profinite completion functor, being left Quillen from spaces to profinite spaces, sends these weak homotopy equivalences to weak equivalences in the model category of profinite spaces.
Applying profinite completion levelwise thus gives

\widehat{|\textnormal{B}\textnormal{N}\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}|}\ \simeq\ \widehat{\textnormal{N}\mathsf{S}}_{0},

and hence

\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}\ \cong\ \pi_{0}\,\mathbb{R}\operatorname{End}\bigl(\widehat{\textnormal{N}\mathsf{S}}_{0}\bigr),

as required.
∎

Corollary 5.12
.

The absolute Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
acts through
homotopy automorphisms on the profinite cyclic
\infty
-operad

\textnormal{N}\mathsf{S}_{0}
.

6.
Galois actions on tangles

In this section we explain how the Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
acts on the category of tangles and how this action recovers the Galois actions of Furusho
[
28
]
. We begin by recalling the category of profinite tangles (in Section
6.1
) and then express it operadically as the envelope of the (profinite completion of the) cyclic ribbon operad
\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
(in Section
6.2
). The natural action of
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
on
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is then shown to induce an action on profinite tangles (in Section
6.3
).

6.1.
The ribbon category of tangles

A
tangle
is the image of a proper embedding of finitely many copies of the interval
I=[0,1]
and
S^{1}
in the cube
[-1,1]^{3}
such that the boundary points lie on the faces
[-1,1]\times\{0\}\times\{\pm 1\}
. These endpoints are typically arranged in a prescribed order along these boundary faces.

In
[
65
]
, Turaev introduced a strict tensor category whose morphisms are the isotopy classes of framed, oriented tangles
\mathbf{T}
whose objects are words

w\in\mathbb{M}(\{+,-\})
and whose morphisms
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{T}}(w,w^{\prime})
are isotopy classes of framed, oriented tangles with
\operatorname{source}(T)=w
and
\operatorname{target}(T)=w^{\prime}
. Composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{T}}(w,w^{\prime})\times\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{T}}(w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{T}}(w,w^{\prime\prime})}

is given by vertical stacking, and the monoidal product
\otimes\colon\mathbf{T}\times\mathbf{T}\to\mathbf{T}
by horizontal juxtaposition of tangles. The unit object in
\mathbf{T}
is the empty word
\emptyset
. The category
\mathbf{T}
admits duals, where the dual of an object
w=(+-++-)
is defined by reversing the word and flipping each sign:
w^{*}=(+--+-)
.

The category
\mathbf{T}
admits a finite presentation (cf.
[
49
, Chapter XII.2]
). It is generated by the
cup
(coevaluation) and
cap
(evaluation) morphisms

\mathrm{coev}\colon\emptyset\to(-,+),\qquad\mathrm{ev}\colon(+,-)\to\emptyset;

the
braiding
morphism

c_{+,+}\colon(+,+)\to(+,+),

and its inverse. Because we work with framed tangles, there is also a
twist

\theta_{+}\colon+\to+.
These generators are subject to the Reidemeister relations,

(c_{+,+}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes c_{+,+})(c_{+,+}\otimes\mathrm{id})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes c_{+,+})(c_{+,+}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes c_{+,+}),

the hexagon and naturality relations for
c_{+,+}
, the snake identities for
\mathrm{ev}
and
\mathrm{coev}
:

(\mathrm{coev}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{+})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{+}\otimes\mathrm{ev})=\mathrm{id}_{+}\quad\text{and}\quad(\mathrm{id}_{-}\otimes\mathrm{coev})\circ(\mathrm{ev}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{-})=\mathrm{id}_{-}

and the twist–braiding compatibility

c_{X,Y}\circ c_{Y,X}=(\theta_{X}\otimes\theta_{Y})\,\theta_{X\otimes Y}^{-1}.

Together these relations present
\mathbf{T}
as the free strict ribbon monoidal category on one object (
[
63
]
,
[
48
, Section 3.5]
). Duals are obtained by reversing the orientation of strands and composing them with the cup and cap morphisms.

The category
q\mathbf{T}
of
q
-tangles
(or
quantum tangles
) is a non-strict refinement of the strict ribbon category
\mathbf{T}
, which plays an important role in the construction of universal finite type invariants of tangles (cf.
[
5
]
,
[
50
]
,
[
45
]
). The objects of
q\mathbf{T}
are sequences of
parenthesized
words in the symbols
+
and
-
. The morphisms in
q\mathbf{T}
are the same as those in
\mathbf{T}
, but the monoidal structure is now only associative up to a specified isomorphism. In particular, for any objects
s,t,u\in\Omega(+,-)
, there is a distinguished associator isomorphism

(s\otimes t)\otimes u\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s,t,u}}s\otimes(t\otimes u).

6.2.
Profinite tangles

Furusho introduced in
[
28
]
a profinite analogue of Turaev’s category of framed, oriented tangles,
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}
, whose morphisms are isotopy classes of profinite tangle diagrams.

A profinite framed tangle diagram is obtained by composing three families of elementary pieces:
annihilations
,
profinite ribbon braids
, and
creations
subject to the natural compatibility of source and target orientations. In other words, a framed profinite tangle diagram is an element of a profinite ribbon braid group
\widehat{\beta}\in\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n}
to which we have formally adjoined cups and caps. The following is a framed version of
[
28
, Definition 2.2]
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
A
,
\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n}
, and
C
denote the sets of

annihilations
,
profinite braids
, and
creations
, defined as

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\{\,a^{\epsilon}_{k,l}\mid k,l\geq 0,\epsilon\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}^{k}\times\{\underset{\rightarrow}{\cap},\underset{\leftarrow}{\cap}\}\times\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}^{l}\,\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{\,b^{\epsilon}_{n}=(b_{n},\epsilon)\mid b_{n}\in\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n},\epsilon\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}^{n},n\geq 1\,\},

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\{\,c^{\epsilon}_{k,l}\mid k,l\geq 0,\epsilon\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}^{k}\times\{\underset{\rightarrow}{\cup},\underset{\leftarrow}{\cup}\}\times\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}^{l}\,\}.

A
profinite framed tangle diagram
is a finite sequence

T=\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{n},\qquad\gamma_{i}\in A\cup\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n}\cup C,

such that the target orientation of
\gamma_{i}
coincides with the source orientation of
\gamma_{i+1}
for each
i
. Two profinite tangles are said to be
isotopic
if one can be transformed into the other by a finite sequence of continuous deformations (Reidemeister moves) compatible with the profinite topology, as defined in
[
28
, Definition 2.9]
.

Definition 6.2
.

The framed
profinite tangle category

\widehat{\mathbf{T}}
has the same objects as the tangle category
\mathbf{T}
, namely signed words in the set
\{+,-\}
. The morphisms

\operatorname{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}(w,w^{\prime})

are the isotopy classes of framed profinite tangle diagrams with source
w
and target
w^{\prime}
.
6
6

6

Note that because each morphism space
\operatorname{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}(w,w^{\prime})
is a subset of the union
A\cup\widehat{\mathsf{RB}}_{n}\cup C
for some
n\geq 1
it is a profinite set or a compact, totally disconnected, Hausdorff space. See also
[
28
, Note 2.10]
for the profinite topology on
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}
.

Composition in
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}
is given by vertical stacking of profinite tangles, and the tensor product
\otimes
by horizontal juxtaposition. Both operations are continuous, as are the structural morphisms for braiding, twist, and duality. In this sense
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}
is a strict ribbon category enriched in profinite sets.

The prop associated to the operad
\mathsf{CoRB}
is the strict symmetric monoidal category whose objects are the natural numbers,
\operatorname{ob}(\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB}))=\mathbb{N}
, and whose morphism sets are defined by

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})}(p,q):=\coprod\limits_{\Sigma q_{i}=q}\bigl(\mathsf{CoRB}(q_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathsf{CoRB}(q_{p})\bigr)\times_{\Sigma_{q_{1}}\times\cdots\times\Sigma_{q_{p}}}\Sigma_{q},

where the coproduct runs over all
p
-tuples of nonnegative integers
(q_{1},\dots,q_{p})
satisfying
\sum_{i}q_{i}=q
.

It is known that algebras over
\mathsf{CoRB}
are precisely strict balanced monoidal categories
[
66
, Proposition 2.11]
. Equivalently,
\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})
is isomorphic to the free strict balanced braided monoidal category generated by one object. Passing to the cyclic operad
\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
formally adjoins duals to such algebras.

Let
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{T}
be full subcategory of tangles which satisfy a strict self-duality relation
(+)^{*}=+
. Topologically, the self-duality relation corresponds to identifying each oriented strand with its dual, effectively making orientation reversal invisible. This simplifies the duality structure: cups and caps become symmetric pairs, and evaluation and coevaluation maps now go from
+\otimes+
to the unit object and back.

We write
\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
for the cyclic operad obtained by aritywise profinite completion of
\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
, and
\Pi\big((\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}})^{\mathrm{cyc}}\big)
for its associated metric prop in profinite sets, in which all pairings and axioms are interpreted continuously.

Proposition 6.3
.

There is an isomorphism of profinite monoidal categories

\Pi\big((\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}})^{\mathrm{cyc}}\big)\cong\ \widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime},

where
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
denotes the profinite completion of the strict ribbon subcategory of framed, oriented tangles with self-dual objects.

Proof.

We first define a strict monoidal functor
F\colon\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})\to\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
. Note that, since the envelop construction
\operatorname{Env}:\mathbf{Op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Prop}
is fully faithful, specifying a symmetric monoidal functor
F\colon\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})\to\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
is equivalent to giving a morphism of operads
\rho\colon\mathsf{CoRB}\to\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{\mathsf{Cat}}}(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime})
.
The assignment in (
6.1
) defines such a map by sending the generators of
\mathsf{CoRB}
to the corresponding structural morphisms of
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
:

(6.1)

F(1)=+,\qquad F(\beta_{1,2})=c_{+,+},\qquad\text{and}\qquad F(\tau_{1})=\theta_{+},

where
c_{+,+}
and
\theta_{+}
denote the braiding, associator, and twist in
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
, respectively. Because
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
is a strict, balanced monoidal category, it follows from
[
66
, Proposition 2.11]
that the coherence axioms for the braiding and balancing operations ensure that the defining relations of
\mathsf{CoRB}
are preserved. Indeed, because the coherence relations for the braiding and balancing in
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
correspond to the isotopy relations on profinite tangles, one can show that, for any
p,q\in\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})
, the functor
F
induces a bijection

(6.2)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}})}(p,q)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}}\bigl(F(p),F(q)\bigr).

Now consider the metric prop
\Pi(\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
, obtained by adjoining the distinguished cup and cap morphisms
b\in\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}})
and
d\in\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{CoRB}})(2,0)
satisfying the zig–zag and equivariance relations of Definition
2.10
.
The functor
F
extends to a functor of props

F^{\prime}\colon\Pi(\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}

by setting
F^{\prime}(b)=\mathrm{coev}
and
F^{\prime}(d)=\mathrm{ev}
, the standard cup and cap morphisms in
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
. The zig–zag relations (
2.5
)–(
2.6
) in
\Pi(\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
correspond to the creation-annihilation relation T
6
in
[
28
, Definition 2.8]
in
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
as part of the duality structure. The remaining cyclic equivariance relation

(\operatorname{id}\otimes b)(\operatorname{id}\otimes f\otimes\operatorname{id})(d\otimes\operatorname{id})=z^{*}f,\qquad f\in\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}(n),

is precisely the categorical formula for the transpose of a morphism in a tensor category with duality (
[
49
, Chapter XIV.2]
): for
f\colon X\to Y
, one has

f^{*}=(\operatorname{id}_{Y^{*}}\otimes b_{X})(\operatorname{id}_{Y^{*}}\otimes f\otimes\operatorname{id}_{X^{*}})(d_{Y}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{X^{*}}).

Hence, the cyclic action
z^{*}
in the operad
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
encodes the assignment
f\mapsto f^{*}
, that is, rotation of morphisms under the duality pairing. Since this operation is built into the ribbon structure of
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
, the functor
F^{\prime}
automatically preserves it.

Since
F^{\prime}
is fully faithful by (
6.2
) and essentially surjective (both categories have the same objects), it follows that
F^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of monoidal categories.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Proposition
6.3
connects to several related results in the literature.
For example, Shum
[
63
]
showed that the tangle category is a universal strict ribbon monoidal category. See also
[
49
, Theorem XIV.5.1]
. In more recent work,
[
57
, Theorem 5.6]
established that algebras over the cyclic operad

\mathsf{CoRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
correspond to ribbon monoidal categories equipped with a compatible self-duality.
From this perspective, our proposition can be understood as identifying the tangle category
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
as the
universal strict ribbon monoidal category with self-dual objects.

The envelope
\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{PaRB})
differs from
\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{CoRB})
only at the level of objects.

In the presence of a nontrivial associator
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}(3)
, implies that in the rigid tensor category
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
the zig-zag equations must additional compatibility conditions ensuring coherence with associativity. Explicitly, the following composites

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\cong 0\otimes 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes(1\otimes 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes 0\cong 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b\otimes\operatorname{id}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{1}\otimes d}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\cong 1\otimes 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes(1\otimes 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1\otimes 1)\otimes 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\otimes 1\cong 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}_{1}\otimes b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\otimes\operatorname{id}_{1}}

are required to equal the identity morphism in
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})(1,1)
. Moreover, (
2.7
) in
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
translates to expressing that cyclic rotation in a rigid monoidal category corresponds to taking the transpose of a morphism with respect to the duality.

Corollary 6.5
.

The metric prop
\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
associated to the cyclic operad
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is isomorphic, as a tensor category, to the category
q\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{\prime}
of parenthesized profinite tangles with self-dual objects.

Remark 6.6
.

Furusho
[
28
, Remark 2.43]
observes that in the profinite
tangle category
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}
, certain composites of cups and caps
appearing in the zig–zag identities, such as the morphism

\Lambda_{f}^{-1}
, need not be invertible a priori.
Consequently, inverses of these morphisms can only be obtained
after localizing to the fraction category

\mathrm{Frac}\,\widehat{\mathcal{K}}
.
In contrast, our construction avoids this issue since, when
working with the metric prop
\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
, we
automatically adjoin cup and cap morphisms in such a way that
every object becomes
self-dual
.
The evaluation and coevaluation morphisms are thus genuine inverses
within the category, not merely up to localization.
Accordingly, the subcategory

\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\simeq q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
of
self-dual framed tangles forms a well-defined prop in profinite
groupoids, and the induced
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
–action constructed above is defined
internally, without passing to a fraction or completion.

6.3.
Galois actions on profinite tangles

In this section we aim to extend the action of the profinite Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}

from the cyclic operad
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
to the profinite tangle category
q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
.
To do so, we first observe that the action of
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
on the operad
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}

extends naturally to an action on its envelope.
Indeed, combining
[
43
]
with
[
13
, Prop. 7.7]

gives natural identifications of profinite groups

\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Op}}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaB}})\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Op}}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})\cong\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}.

Since the envelope functor
\operatorname{Env}\colon\mathbf{Op}\to\mathbf{Prop}
is fully faithful, any operad automorphism
F\colon\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}\to\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}

induces a unique automorphism of props

\operatorname{Env}(F)\colon\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}),

and vice versa. In particular, for a pair
(\lambda,f)\in\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
, the corresponding automorphism
F
acts on the generators of the prop
\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})
by

F(\mu)=\mu,\qquad F(\beta)=\beta^{\lambda},\qquad F(\tau)=\tau^{\lambda},\qquad F(\alpha)=f(x_{12},x_{23})\cdot\alpha,

where
f
is a word in the profinite pure braid group
\widehat{\mathsf{PB}}_{3}
.

We summarize these observations in the following proposition, which may be regarded as a ribbon-braid analogue of
[
4
, §2]
.

Proposition 6.7
.

There are natural isomorphisms of profinite groups

\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Op}}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Prop}}(\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})).

∎

To extend the
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
–action from the cyclic operad
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}

to the associated metric prop, we first recall that
\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
is obtained from
\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})
by adjoining distinguished morphisms

b\in\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})(0,2)\qquad\text{and}\qquad d\in\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})(2,0),

called the
cup
and
cap
, subject to the zig–zag and cyclic equivariance relations of Definition
2.10
.

Proposition 6.8
.

The
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
–action on the cyclic operad
\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
extends to an action on the metric prop
\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
.

Proof.

Given an
(\lambda,f)\in\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
we aim to define an automorphism
F^{\prime}:\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\rightarrow\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
. As discussed above,
(\lambda,f)
defines a unique automorphism of props
\operatorname{Env}(F)\colon\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Env}(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}})
which acts on the generators of
\mathsf{PaRB}
by

F(\mu)=\mu,\qquad F(\beta)=\beta^{\lambda},\qquad F(\tau)=\tau^{\lambda},\qquad F(\alpha)=f(x_{12},x_{23})\cdot\alpha.

Following
[
48
, Appendix D]
, we can extend
F
to
F^{\prime}\colon\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\longrightarrow\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
by setting

F^{\prime}(b)=b,\quad\text{and}\quad F^{\prime}(d)=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\nu)d,

where

\nu=\big((b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big)^{-1}.

To check that
F^{\prime}
is well defined we must show that
F^{\prime}
preseves the two zig-zag equations

(ZZ1)

(b\otimes\operatorname{id})\,\alpha\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)=\operatorname{id},

(ZZ2)

(\operatorname{id}\otimes b)\,\alpha^{-1}\,(d\otimes\operatorname{id})=\operatorname{id}

and cyclic equivarience.

Applying
F^{\prime}
to the left hand side of (
ZZ1
) gives

\displaystyle F^{\prime}\big((b\otimes\operatorname{id})\,\alpha\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bigl(F^{\prime}(b)\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,F(\alpha)\,\operatorname{id}\otimes F^{\prime}(d)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,f(x_{12},x_{23})\,\alpha\,\operatorname{id}\otimes(\operatorname{id}\otimes\nu)d\big).

Substituting
\nu=((b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d))^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}\big((b\otimes\operatorname{id})\,\alpha\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big)

\displaystyle=\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,f(x_{12},x_{23})\,\alpha\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\operatorname{id}\otimes\big[(b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big]^{-1})d\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,f(x_{12},x_{23})\,\alpha\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\big[(b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big]^{-1}\big)d\big)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\cong}\big(\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,f(x_{12},x_{23})\,\alpha\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\big[(b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big]^{-1}\big)d\big)

\displaystyle\overset{(2)}{\cong}\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\circ\,\alpha\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}^{-1}\big)\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes d\big)

\displaystyle\cong\bigl(b\otimes\operatorname{id}\bigr)\,\alpha\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes d\big)

\displaystyle\overset{(3)}{=}\operatorname{id}.

Where
(1)
follows because
\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\big[(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)^{-1}f(x_{12},x_{23})^{-1}(b\otimes\operatorname{id})^{-1}\big]\big)
is an automorphism in
\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{PaRB}^{cyc}(3)}((12)3)\cong\widehat{\mathsf{PB}}_{3}
we can pull the
f(x_{12},x_{23})^{-1}
to the left. The equivalences
(2)
and
(3)
follow from (
ZZ1
). That
F^{\prime}
preserves the second zig-zag equation (
ZZ2
) follows a similar argument.
∎

Remark 6.9
.

Kassel-Turaev in
[
48
, Appendix D]
also provides an equivalent assignment on duality pairing
(d,b)
. We note that the same assignment on the prop map
F^{\prime}
for the duality pairing
(d,b)
, that is, as follows

F^{\prime}(b)=b,\quad F^{\prime}(d)=(\rho\otimes\operatorname{id})d,

where
\rho=\left((\operatorname{id}\otimes b)\alpha^{-1}f(x_{12},x_{23})^{-1}(d\otimes\operatorname{id})\right)^{-1}
gives a
\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}
action. In this case, the duality pairing must satisfy the relation
(b\otimes\operatorname{id})\alpha^{-1}(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\cong\operatorname{id}
under the
\underline{\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}}
action. The verification is similar to the one in Proposition
6.8
.

Corollary 6.10
.

Via the identification

\Pi(\widehat{\mathsf{PaRB}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})\cong q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
,
the Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
acts by
automorphisms on the profinite tangle category
q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
.
In particular, the composite

\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}\longrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}(q\mathbf{T}^{\prime})

defines a nontrivial Galois action on
q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
.

7.
Turaev’s tangle category and rational formality of the little disks operad

The tangle category of
[
48
]
is a
\mathbb{K}
-linear version of the category described in Section
6.1
. The tangle category described in
[
48
, Section 2.2]
is a prounipotently complete
\mathbb{K}
-linear version of the tangle category
\mathbf{T}
, which we will denote by
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{K}}
. We begin with a review of the prounipotent completion of categories, operads, and props in Section
7.1
, but in practice this means that morphisms in
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{K}}
are formal linear combinations of morphisms in
\mathbf{T}
. We let
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{K}}
be the full subcategory of self dual objects. We will show that this
\widehat{\mathsf{GT}}
-action descends to the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
-action on prounipotent tangles as described in Kassel–Turaev
[
48
, Appendix D]
in Section
7.2
. Finally, we exploit the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
-action to prove the rational formality of the cyclic framed little disk operad in Section
7.3
.

7.1.
Prounipotent completion of categories, operads, and props

We briefly recall the notion of prounipotent completion following
[
48
, Section 3]
and
[
26
, Chapter 9]
.
Let
\mathsf{G}
be a group and let
\mathbb{K}
be a field of characteristic zero. The
prounipotent completion
of
\mathsf{G}
over
\mathbb{K}
is the group of grouplike elements in the

completed group algebra

\widehat{\mathbb{K}[\mathsf{G}]}:=\lim_{n}\mathbb{K}[\mathsf{G}]/I^{n},

where
I=\ker(\mathbb{K}[\mathsf{G}]\to\mathbb{K})
is the augmentation ideal. We will write
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbb{K}}=\mathrm{gr}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{K}[\mathsf{G}]}\big)
for the grouplike elements.
The assignment
\mathsf{G}\mapsto\mathsf{G}_{\mathbb{K}}
defines a functor

(-)_{\mathbb{K}}:\mathbf{Gr}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Gr}_{\mathbb{K}},

and coincides with the standard prounipotent completion described in
[
26
, Chapter 8]
.

Example 7.1
.

For the braid group
\mathsf{PB}_{n}
, the prounipotent completion
(\mathsf{PB}_{n})_{\mathbb{K}}
consists of the grouplike elements of the completed group algebra
\lim_{n}\mathbb{K}[\mathsf{PB}_{n}]/I^{n}
, where
I=\langle x_{ij}-1\rangle
and
x_{ij}
are the standard generators.

7.1.1.
Prounipotent completion of
\mathbb{K}
–linear monoidal categories

The notion of prounipotent completion for groups extends naturally to categories, operads, and props. Let
\mathbf{M}
be a category and let
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
be its
\mathbb{K}
-linearization, which is the
\mathbb{K}
–linear category generated by
\mathbf{M}
. This means that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]}(x,y)=\mathbb{K}[\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{M}}(x,y)]
is the
\mathbb{K}
vector space generated by
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{M}}(x,y)
. An
ideal

\mathbf{I}\subset\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
is a
\mathbb{K}
–linear subcategory closed under composition and tensor product, i.e.
f\circ g
and
f\otimes g
lie in
\mathbf{I}
whenever
f\in\mathbf{I}
or
g\in\mathbf{I}
.
We define the
augmentation ideal

\mathbf{I}=\ker\big(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]}(-,-)\longrightarrow\mathbb{K}\big),

as the kernel of the augmentation (or counit) functor sending
\operatorname{id}_{X}\mapsto 1\in\mathbb{K}
and sends all other
f\mapsto 0
. This has the effect that, for any
x\in\mathbf{M}
, the map
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]}(X,X)\longrightarrow\mathbb{K}
sends any linear combination
\Sigma_{i}\lambda_{i}f_{i}\mapsto\Sigma_{i}\lambda_{i}
.
Let
\mathbf{I}^{n+1}
denote the ideal generated by all composites of at least
n{+}1
elements of
\mathbf{I}
.

Example 7.2
.

If
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
is the linearization of a
braided monoidal
category, the ideal
\mathbf{I}
is generated by the

braiding defect

c_{X,Y}c_{Y,X}-\operatorname{id}_{X\otimes Y}
, and
\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{K}}
is the limit of the resulting filtration.
If
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
is a
ribbon
category, the same construction applies to the underlying braided structure, with the
additional relation
\Theta_{X}^{2}-\operatorname{id}_{X}\in\mathbf{I}
, reflecting compatibility with the twist and duality morphisms

[
48
, Lemma 3.4]
.

The
prounipotent completion
of
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
is the inverse limit of the quotient categories by successive powers of the augmentation ideal,

\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{K}}:=\lim_{n}\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]/\mathbf{I}^{\,n+1},

where the quotient category
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]/\mathbf{I}^{\,n+1}
has the same objects as
\mathbf{M}
and

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]/\mathbf{I}^{\,n+1}}(X,Y)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]}(X,Y)/\mathbf{I}^{\,n+1}(X,Y).

Equivalently,
\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{K}}
is obtained by completing
\mathbb{K}[\mathbf{M}]
with respect to its augmentation ideal filtration.

Remark 7.3
.

The completion
\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{K}}
can often be interpreted as an
\hbar
–adic deformation of
\mathbf{M}
.
When the ideal
\mathbf{I}
is generated by a first–order infinitesimal such as
c_{X,Y}c_{Y,X}-\operatorname{id}
, the prounipotent completion is equivalent to the formal power–series category
\mathbf{M}[[\hbar]]
under suitable identifications.

7.1.2.
Prounipotent completion of operads and props

Prounipotent completion of groups and categories defines a strong monoidal functor, in the sense that it preserves monoidal products up to coherent isomorphism. In particular, one can apply it entrywise to operads (or cyclic operads) in groups or groupoids:

(-)_{\mathbb{K}}:\mathbf{Op}(\mathbf{Gr})\longrightarrow\mathbf{Op}(\mathbf{Gr}_{\mathbb{K}}),

as shown in
[
26
, Prop. 9.2.2]
.

Definition 7.4
.

For an operad
\mathcal{O}=\{\mathcal{O}(n)\}_{n\geq 0}
in groupoids, its
prounipotent completion
is the operad

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{K}}:=\{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{K}}(n)\}_{n\geq 0},

obtained by applying
(-)_{\mathbb{K}}
to each arity. If
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is a cyclic operad, its completion
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}}
is defined analogously and inherits the cyclic structure of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
.

Since the prounipotent completion is symmetric monoidal, it also commutes with the envelope and metric–prop constructions:

Proposition 7.5
.

For any operad
\mathcal{O}
, there is a natural isomorphism
\operatorname{Env}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathbb{K}}\cong\operatorname{Env}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{K}}).

Similarly, for any cyclic operad
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
, one has a canonical isomorphism
\Pi(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cyc}})_{\mathbb{K}}\cong\Pi({\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}}).
∎

7.2.
Turaev’s Tangle Category

In this section, we describe the prounipotent
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
action on the prounipotent category of tangles. This Section is parallel to Section
6.3
in prounipotent completions.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be a field of characteristic zero. Let
\mathsf{F}(x,y)_{\mathbb{K}}
denote the prounipotent completion of the free group
\mathsf{F}
on two generators
x
and
y
over
\mathbb{K}
. Given an element
f\in\mathsf{F}(x,y)_{\mathbb{K}}
and a group homomorphism
\gamma:\mathsf{F}(x,y)_{\mathbb{K}}\to\widehat{\mathsf{G}}_{\mathbb{K}}
into a prounipotent group
\mathsf{G}
, we write
f(a,b)
for the image
\gamma(f)
, where
\gamma(x)=a
and
\gamma(y)=b
.

Definition 7.6

(
[
20
]
)
.

The
\mathbb{K}
-prounipotent Grothendieck–Teichmüller monoid
\underline{\mathsf{GT}}(\mathbb{K})
consists of pairs
(\lambda,f)\in\mathbb{K}\times\mathsf{F}(x,y)_{\mathbb{K}}
satisfying:

(1)

f(x,y)=f(y,x)
,

(2)

x^{\mu}f(x,y)y^{\mu}f(y,z)z^{\mu}f(z,x)=1
in
\mathsf{F}(x,y)_{\mathbb{K}}
, where
xyz=1
and
\lambda=2\mu+1
,

(3)

f(x_{12},x_{23})f(x_{12}x_{13},x_{24}x_{34})f(x_{23},x_{34})=f(x_{13}x_{23},x_{23})f(x_{12},x_{23}x_{24})
in
({\mathsf{PB}}_{4})_{\mathbb{K}}
.

The group
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
is defined as the set of invertible elements in
\underline{\mathsf{GT}}(\mathbb{K})
, with group law:

(\lambda_{1},f_{1})*(\lambda_{2},f_{2})=\left(\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2},\ f_{1}\big(f_{2}(x,y)^{-1}x^{\lambda_{2}}f_{2}(x,y),\ y^{\lambda_{2}}\big)\cdot f_{2}(x,y)\right).

By
[
26
, Theorem 11.1.7]
, the prounipotent analogue of

[
13
, Proposition 7.11]
, and Lemma
5.2
,
we have the following result. The graded version of this statement also appeared in
[
68
, Corollary 1.5]
.

Theorem 7.7
.

There are natural isomorphisms of prounipotent groups

\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Op}}(\mathsf{PaB}_{\mathbb{K}})\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Op}}(\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{K}})\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Cyc}}(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}})\cong\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K}).

As in the profinite case, these identifications extend to props, giving

\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Prop}}(\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{PaB}_{\mathbb{K}}))\cong\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathbf{Prop}}(\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{K}}))\cong\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K}).

This result, originally observed in
[
4
]
, implies that the prounipotent Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
acts by prop automorphisms on
\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{K}})
through maps

F(\mu)=\mu,\qquad F(\beta)=\beta^{\lambda},\qquad F(\tau)=\tau^{\lambda},\qquad F(\alpha)=f(x_{12},x_{23})\cdot\alpha,

for
(\lambda,f)\in\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
.

The metric prop
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}})
is obtained from
\operatorname{Env}(\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{K}})
by adjoining duality morphisms

b\in\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}})(0,2)\qquad\text{and}\qquad d\in\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}})(2,0),

satisfying the standard zig–zag identities. Following
[
48
, Appendix D]
, the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
–action extends uniquely to this metric prop by defining

F^{\prime}(b)=b,\qquad F^{\prime}(d)=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\nu)d,\qquad\text{where}\quad\nu=\big((b\otimes\operatorname{id})f(x_{12},x_{23})\alpha(\operatorname{id}\otimes d)\big)^{-1}.

A direct verification, similar to Proposition
6.8
, shows that
F^{\prime}
preserves both zig–zag relations and acts trivially on single-strand tangles, yielding the following result.

Proposition 7.8
.

The
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
-action on
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}}
extends to an action on the metric prop
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{K}})
.

The following result is the prounipotent version of Proposition
6.3
and Corollary
6.5
.

Corollary 7.9
.

The metric prop
\Pi(\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{K}}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
associated to the cyclic operad
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is isomorphic, as a tensor category, to the category
q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
of parenthesized prounipotent tangles with self-dual objects.

The Proposition
7.8
and Corollary
7.9
imply that the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{K})
acts on the category
q\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
.

Remark 7.10
.

Given the
\ell
-adic completions
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
, for any prime
\ell
, the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
–action induces a
\mathsf{Gal}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
action on the prounipotent category
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{Q_{\ell}}}
of tangles ,

\mathsf{Gal}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\longrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{Q_{\ell}}}.

This Galois action, which passes through operadic machinery, extends to knots and links, as constructed by Kassel-Turaev in
[
48
, Appendix D]
.

7.3.
Cyclic formality of the framed little disks operad

In this section, we prove the rational formality of the cyclic operad
\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
of framed little disks using the explicit cyclic structure on
\mathsf{PaRB}
(Section
3.3
) and the action of the prounipotent Grothendieck–Teichmüller group
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
.

An operad
\mathcal{O}
is said to be
formal
if there exists a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of dg-operads connecting its chain operad
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}(\mathcal{O})
to its homology operad
H_{\ast}(\mathcal{O})
. Rational formality of certain moduli space operads has been previously established—for example, Giansiracusa and Salvatore
[
31
]
proved the formality of the cyclic operad of genus-zero moduli spaces. Campos, Idrissi, and Willwacher
[
68
, Theorem 3.1]
later showed that
\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is also formal using cyclic graph complex techniques.

We provide an alternative proof of this result using the action of
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
, following a strategy inspired by Boavida–Horel–Robertson
[
13
]
and formalised through Petersen’s formality criterion
[
59
]
. Specifically, we show that the morphism
\mathsf{PaRB}\to\mathsf{PaRB}_{\mathbb{Q}}
lifts compatibly to the cyclic setting, yielding a map
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\to\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
that respects the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
-action. Applying Petersen’s criterion gives the desired formality.

Example 7.11
.

The Batalin–-Vilkovisky operad
\mathsf{BV}
is a differential graded operad whose algebras are precisely Batalin-–Vilkovisky algebras, in particular, a quadruple
(A,\wedge,[-,-],\Delta,)
in which
A
is a graded vector space,
\wedge
is a graded commutative product in degree
0
,
[-,-]
is a lie bracket on
A
of degree
-1
, and
\Delta:A\rightarrow A
is a BV operator of degree
-1
such that
\wedge
makes
A
into a graded commutative algebra together with the following relations:

(7.1)

\Delta^{2}=0\ \ \text{and}\ \ [x,y\wedge z]=[x,y]\wedge z+(-1)^{(|x|+1)|y|}y\wedge[x,z],

(7.2)

[x,y]=\Delta(x\wedge y)-(\Delta x)\wedge y-(-1)^{|x|}x\wedge(\Delta y)

The operad
\mathsf{BV}
generated by the graded commutative algebra product
\wedge
in arity
2
, and the differential operator
\Delta
in arity
1
. Note that the bracket
[-,-]
is implicitly given by the second relation (
7.2
), which reduces the number of generators to two. The operad
\mathsf{BV}
can be equivalently defined as the homology operad of the framed little
2
-disk operad,
H_{*}(\mathsf{FD}_{1})
and
\mathsf{BV}
admits a cyclic structure. See, for example,
[
30
]
for more details.

Proposition 7.12

(
[
59
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a dg operad over a field
\mathbb{K}
of characteristic zero. If a grading automorphism of the homology operad
H_{\ast}(\mathcal{O})
lifts to an automorphism of
\mathcal{O}
, then
\mathcal{O}
is formal.

The following result can be seen as an extension of the formality of the operad
\mathsf{FD}_{2}
from
[
13
, Theorem 9.1]
to its cyclic version in the prounipotent case.

Theorem 7.13
.

The cyclic operad of framed little disks
\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
is rationally formal.

Proof.

This is by definition that
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathsf{PaRB}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
. We know that, by taking the realization of the classifying space
|\textnormal{B}(\mathsf{PaRB}^{cyc})|
we have a topological operad which is homotopy equivalent to the operad
\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{cyc}
. As such, it acts on cyclic dg-operad of singular chains
\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathrm{cyc}},\mathbb{Q})
. Recall that the homology operad
H_{*}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathrm{cyc}})
is the operad of Batalin-Vilkovisky algebras, denoted by
\mathsf{BV}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{BV}
admits a cyclic structure induced by the cyclic structure of the topological operad
\mathcal{M}
, the genus zero surface operad. Since
\mathsf{BV}
is generated by the commutative algebra product
[-,-]
in arity 2 and the operator
\Delta
in arity 1, it is enough to check the
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
action on the homology groups
H_{0}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}(2))
and
H_{1}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}(1))
.
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
action is trivial on
H_{0}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}(2))
due to the assignment
\beta\longrightarrow\beta^{\lambda}
. The action extends to
H_{0}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}(2))
, since
z_{3}\cdot\beta^{\lambda}=(z_{3}\cdot\beta)^{\lambda}
, See Lemma
5.2
. There is a map

\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}(H_{*}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}))

that determines an automorphism
\gamma_{\lambda}\in\operatorname{Aut}(H_{n}(FD))
given by multiplication
\lambda^{n}
, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
. The prounipotent completion of
\mathbb{Z}
over the field
\mathbb{K}
of characteristic 0 is
\mathbb{K}
, therefore we have
\mathsf{PRB}(1)_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathbb{Q}
. Since the cyclic action on
\mathsf{PRB}(1)
is trivial, it follows that
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
H_{1}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}(1))
via
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}(H_{1}(\mathsf{FD}_{2}^{\mathsf{cyc}}(1)))
, where the first map sends the pair
(\lambda,f)
to
\lambda
.
Now, using the fact that the cyclotomic character
\mathsf{GT}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
is surjective and Petersen’s formality criteria in
[
59
]
, we prove the claim.
∎
